   → Việc mất môi trường sống góp phần làm suy giảm nhiều loài thụ phấn.

2. **Several research teams contributed data to the international study.**
   → Một số nhóm nghiên cứu đã đóng góp dữ liệu cho nghiên cứu quốc tế.

##### Common collocations

* **contribute to climate change**: góp phần gây biến đổi khí hậu
* **contribute to understanding**: góp phần nâng cao hiểu biết
* **make a contribution**: có sự đóng góp
* **a significant contribution**: một đóng góp đáng kể

##### Synonyms

* **help cause** /help kɔːz/ *(verb phrase)*: góp phần gây ra; rõ nghĩa nhưng ít trang trọng hơn
* **play a part in** /pleɪ ə pɑːt ɪn/ *(verb phrase)*: đóng vai trò trong; nhấn mạnh vai trò chứ không nhất thiết là quan hệ nhân quả
* **lead to** /liːd tuː/ *(phrasal verb)*: dẫn đến; thường gợi mối liên hệ mạnh hơn

##### Word family

* **contribution** /ˌkɒn.trɪˈbjuː.ʃən/ *(countable noun)*: sự đóng góp  
  *Example:* Darwin's work made a major contribution to evolutionary theory.  
  → Công trình của Darwin có đóng góp lớn cho thuyết tiến hoá.

* **contributor** /kənˈtrɪb.jə.tər/ *(countable noun)*: người/yếu tố đóng góp  
  *Example:* Transport is a major contributor to urban air pollution.  
  → Giao thông là một yếu tố lớn gây ô nhiễm không khí đô thị.

##### Notes

Use `contribute to`, not `contribute for`. A contributor may be a person **or a causal factor**. Do not replace `cause` with `contribute to` when the passage claims a single sufficient cause.

---

#### 2. Affect /əˈfekt/ and Effect /ɪˈfekt/

* **Part of speech:** `affect` is mainly a transitive verb; `effect` is mainly a countable/uncountable noun
* **Vietnamese meaning:** `affect`: ảnh hưởng đến; `effect`: tác động, kết quả

##### Usage

`Affect` describes influence on something: `temperature affects growth`. `Effect` names the resulting change: `the effect of temperature on growth`. Academic passages use both to state or test relationships. The rarer verb `effect` means “bring about”, as in `effect change`.

**Common structures:**

* `affect + noun`: ảnh hưởng đến
* `have an effect on + noun`: có tác động đến
* `the effect of A on B`: tác động của A đối với B
* `take effect`: bắt đầu có hiệu lực/tác dụng

##### Examples

1. **Light intensity affects the rate at which plants grow.**
   → Cường độ ánh sáng ảnh hưởng đến tốc độ phát triển của cây.

2. **The long-term effects of the treatment remain uncertain.**
   → Những tác động dài hạn của phương pháp điều trị vẫn chưa chắc chắn.

##### Common collocations

* **adversely affect**: ảnh hưởng bất lợi
* **long-term effect**: tác động dài hạn
* **side effect**: tác dụng phụ
* **cumulative effect**: tác động tích luỹ
* **take effect**: có hiệu lực/tác dụng

##### Synonyms

* **influence** /ˈɪn.flu.əns/ *(verb/noun)*: ảnh hưởng; thường trung tính và có thể gián tiếp
* **impact** /ɪmˈpækt/ *(verb)*; /ˈɪm.pækt/ *(noun)*: tác động; thường gợi tác động đáng kể, nhưng lạm dụng như động từ có thể làm văn phong nặng nề
* **consequence** /ˈkɒn.sɪ.kwəns/ *(noun)*: hệ quả; thường là kết quả có ý nghĩa, đôi khi tiêu cực

##### Word family

* **effective** /ɪˈfek.tɪv/ *(adjective)*: có hiệu quả  
  *Example:* The vaccine is effective against several variants.  
  → Vắc-xin có hiệu quả đối với một số biến thể.

* **effectively** /ɪˈfek.tɪv.li/ *(adverb)*: một cách hiệu quả; trên thực tế  
  *Example:* The barrier effectively prevents salt water from entering the fields.  
  → Rào chắn ngăn nước mặn xâm nhập đồng ruộng một cách hiệu quả.

* **affected** /əˈfek.tɪd/ *(adjective/participle)*: bị ảnh hưởng  
  *Example:* Aid was sent to the affected communities.  
  → Viện trợ đã được gửi đến các cộng đồng bị ảnh hưởng.

##### Notes

The common pattern is verb `affect` versus noun `effect`. Do not confuse `effective` (“successful”) with `efficient` (“achieving a result with little waste”). In psychology, the noun `affect` has a specialist meaning related to observable emotion.

---

## Increase and Decrease

| Direction | Neutral forms | More precise forms |
| --- | --- | --- |
| upward | increase, rise, grow | surge = rise suddenly; soar = rise very rapidly; expand = become larger in scope/size |
| downward | decrease, fall, decline | diminish = become less; shrink = become smaller; plummet = fall extremely quickly |
| unstable | fluctuate, vary | oscillate = move repeatedly between values |
| endpoint | peak, reach a high/low | plateau = stop rising/falling and remain level |
| unchanged | remain stable/constant | level off = become stable after change |

### Core anchor entries

#### 3. Decline /dɪˈklaɪn/

* **Part of speech:** countable/uncountable noun; intransitive verb; transitive verb meaning “refuse”
* **Vietnamese meaning:** sự suy giảm; suy giảm *(80% in academic texts)*; từ chối *(20%)*

##### Usage

As a trend word, `decline` means to become lower in number, quality, strength, or importance. It can describe a process or its amount. Unlike transitive `reduce`, trend-related `decline` normally has no direct object: numbers decline, while a policy reduces numbers.

**Common structures:**

* `decline in + noun`: sự suy giảm về
* `decline by + amount`: giảm một lượng
* `decline from A to B`: giảm từ A xuống B
* `decline to do something`: từ chối làm gì

##### Examples

1. **The population declined by nearly a third after the habitat was fragmented.**
   → Quần thể đã giảm gần một phần ba sau khi môi trường sống bị chia cắt.

2. **Researchers recorded a gradual decline in soil fertility.**
   → Các nhà nghiên cứu ghi nhận sự suy giảm dần về độ phì nhiêu của đất.

##### Common collocations

* **a sharp decline**: sự suy giảm mạnh
* **a steady decline**: sự suy giảm đều đặn
* **population decline**: suy giảm dân số/quần thể
* **fall into decline**: rơi vào tình trạng sa sút

##### Synonyms

* **decrease** /dɪˈkriːs/ *(verb)*; /ˈdiː.kriːs/ *(noun)*: giảm; trung tính nhất
* **diminish** /dɪˈmɪn.ɪʃ/ *(verb)*: giảm dần về mức độ, giá trị hoặc sức mạnh
* **deteriorate** /dɪˈtɪə.ri.ə.reɪt/ *(verb)*: xấu đi về chất lượng/tình trạng, không đơn thuần giảm về lượng

##### Word family

* **declining** /dɪˈklaɪ.nɪŋ/ *(adjective)*: đang suy giảm  
  *Example:* Declining birth rates can alter the age structure of a population.  
  → Tỷ lệ sinh giảm có thể làm thay đổi cơ cấu tuổi của dân số.

##### Notes

Use `a decline in`, but `decline by/from/to`. `A 20% decline` means the amount lost is 20%; `decline to 20%` means the final level is 20%. The refusal sense is transitive: `decline an invitation`.

---

#### 4. Fluctuate /ˈflʌk.tʃu.eɪt/

* **Part of speech:** intransitive verb
* **Vietnamese meaning:** dao động, lên xuống không ổn định

##### Usage

`Fluctuate` describes repeated upward and downward movement in a value, level, price, population, or condition. It does not identify an overall direction unless the context adds one.

**Common structures:**

* `fluctuate between A and B`: dao động giữa A và B
* `fluctuate around + level`: dao động quanh một mức
* `fluctuate according to/with + factor`: dao động theo yếu tố

##### Examples

1. **Water levels fluctuate with seasonal rainfall.**
   → Mực nước dao động theo lượng mưa theo mùa.

2. **The temperature fluctuated between 18 and 27 degrees Celsius.**
   → Nhiệt độ dao động từ 18 đến 27 độ C.

##### Common collocations

* **fluctuate considerably**: dao động đáng kể
* **fluctuating demand**: nhu cầu biến động
* **seasonal fluctuation**: biến động theo mùa
* **price fluctuation**: biến động giá

##### Synonyms

* **vary** /ˈveə.ri/ *(verb)*: thay đổi/khác nhau; rộng hơn, không nhất thiết lên xuống theo thời gian
* **oscillate** /ˈɒs.ɪ.leɪt/ *(verb)*: dao động qua lại giữa hai phía hoặc trạng thái; mang tính kỹ thuật hơn
* **be volatile** /biː ˈvɒl.ə.taɪl/ *(adjective phrase)*: biến động mạnh và khó đoán, thường dùng cho thị trường hoặc điều kiện không ổn định

##### Word family

* **fluctuation** /ˌflʌk.tʃuˈeɪ.ʃən/ *(countable/uncountable noun)*: sự dao động  
  *Example:* Minor fluctuations may reflect measurement error rather than genuine change.  
  → Những dao động nhỏ có thể phản ánh sai số đo lường chứ không phải thay đổi thực sự.

* **fluctuating** /ˈflʌk.tʃu.eɪ.tɪŋ/ *(adjective)*: biến động  
  *Example:* Farmers must cope with fluctuating market prices.  
  → Nông dân phải ứng phó với giá thị trường biến động.

##### Notes

`Fluctuate` is intransitive: say `prices fluctuated`, not `the crisis fluctuated prices`. A graph that rises once and then stays high has **increased**, not fluctuated.

---

## Research and Evidence

| Evidence strength | Typical reporting verbs | Reading implication |
| --- | --- | --- |
| direct display | show, demonstrate, reveal | The writer presents relatively strong support. |
| sign/evidence | indicate, point to | The evidence supports an interpretation without proving it. |
| cautious interpretation | suggest, imply | The conclusion is tentative or indirect. |
| consistency | support, corroborate | New evidence agrees with a claim or earlier evidence. |
| conflict | contradict, challenge, refute | `Refute` is strongest: evidence shows a claim is wrong. |

### Core anchor entry

#### 5. Indicate /ˈɪn.dɪ.keɪt/

* **Part of speech:** transitive verb
* **Vietnamese meaning:** cho thấy, chỉ ra *(evidence, 70%)*; biểu thị/đánh dấu *(30%)*

##### Usage

In academic prose, `indicate` means that evidence, results, or observations point towards a conclusion. It is often deliberately more cautious than `prove`. It can also mean showing a value, direction, or category on an instrument or diagram.

**Common structures:**

* `results/evidence indicate that + clause`: kết quả/bằng chứng cho thấy
* `indicate a change/relationship`: cho thấy sự thay đổi/mối liên hệ
* `as indicated by + noun`: như được cho thấy bởi

##### Examples

1. **The findings indicate that sleep affects the consolidation of memory.**
   → Các phát hiện cho thấy giấc ngủ ảnh hưởng đến quá trình củng cố trí nhớ.

2. **Dark shading on the map indicates areas of high population density.**
   → Phần tô đậm trên bản đồ biểu thị những khu vực có mật độ dân số cao.

##### Common collocations

* **results indicate**: kết quả cho thấy
* **clearly indicate**: cho thấy rõ ràng
* **indicate a trend**: cho thấy một xu hướng
* **indicate the presence of**: cho thấy sự hiện diện của

##### Synonyms

* **suggest** /səˈdʒest/ *(verb)*: gợi ý/cho thấy; thường thận trọng hơn và cho phép nhiều cách giải thích
* **demonstrate** /ˈdem.ən.streɪt/ *(verb)*: chứng minh/cho thấy rõ; thường mạnh hơn `indicate`
* **reveal** /rɪˈviːl/ *(verb)*: tiết lộ/làm lộ ra thông tin trước đó chưa biết

##### Word family

* **indication** /ˌɪn.dɪˈkeɪ.ʃən/ *(countable/uncountable noun)*: dấu hiệu, sự biểu thị  
  *Example:* There is no clear indication that the two events are causally related.  
  → Không có dấu hiệu rõ ràng cho thấy hai sự kiện có quan hệ nhân quả.

* **indicator** /ˈɪn.dɪ.keɪ.tər/ *(countable noun)*: chỉ số, dấu hiệu đo lường  
  *Example:* Infant mortality is often used as an indicator of public health.  
  → Tỷ lệ tử vong ở trẻ sơ sinh thường được dùng làm chỉ số sức khoẻ cộng đồng.

* **indicative** /ɪnˈdɪk.ə.tɪv/ *(adjective)*: cho thấy, mang tính dấu hiệu  
  *Example:* The small sample is not indicative of the whole population.  
  → Mẫu nhỏ này không đại diện cho toàn bộ dân số.

##### Notes

`Indicate` can introduce a `that`-clause without `to`: `the data indicate that...`, not `indicate to that`. Evidence that indicates a conclusion does not necessarily prove causation.

---

## Comparison and Contrast

| Relationship | Common forms | Key grammar/meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| similarity | similarly, likewise, comparable, analogous | `Analogous` means similar in a relevant function, not identical. |
| difference | differ, contrast, distinct, unlike | Use `differ from`; `different from` is safest in formal British English. |
| concession | although, while, whereas, despite | `Despite` takes a noun/gerund; `although` takes a clause. |
| degree | relatively, comparatively, markedly | These calibrate how large the comparison is. |

### Core anchor entry

#### 6. Distinguish /dɪˈstɪŋ.ɡwɪʃ/

* **Part of speech:** transitive verb; intransitive verb with `between`
* **Vietnamese meaning:** phân biệt; nhận ra sự khác nhau

##### Usage

`Distinguish` means to recognise or explain a meaningful difference. IELTS questions may ask whether a study, animal, theory, or process can be distinguished from another. The passive adjective `distinguishable` focuses on whether the difference is detectable.

**Common structures:**

* `distinguish between A and B`: phân biệt A và B
* `distinguish A from B`: phân biệt A với B
* `be distinguished by + feature`: được phân biệt/đặc trưng bởi

##### Examples

1. **The experiment could not distinguish between the effects of age and experience.**
   → Thí nghiệm không thể phân biệt tác động của tuổi tác với tác động của kinh nghiệm.

2. **This species is distinguished from its relatives by the shape of its beak.**
   → Loài này được phân biệt với các loài họ hàng qua hình dạng chiếc mỏ.

##### Common collocations

* **clearly distinguish**: phân biệt rõ
* **distinguishing feature**: đặc điểm phân biệt
* **distinguish fact from opinion**: phân biệt sự thật với ý kiến
* **difficult to distinguish**: khó phân biệt

##### Synonyms

* **differentiate** /ˌdɪf.əˈren.ʃi.eɪt/ *(verb)*: phân biệt/tạo khác biệt; thường trang trọng hoặc kỹ thuật hơn
* **discriminate** /dɪˈskrɪm.ɪ.neɪt/ *(verb)*: nhận biết khác biệt tinh tế; cũng có nghĩa đối xử bất công, nên cần đọc theo ngữ cảnh
* **tell apart** /tel əˈpɑːt/ *(phrasal verb)*: phân biệt; ít trang trọng hơn

##### Word family

* **distinction** /dɪˈstɪŋk.ʃən/ *(countable/uncountable noun)*: sự khác biệt; sự phân biệt  
  *Example:* The passage makes a distinction between correlation and causation.  
  → Bài đọc phân biệt giữa tương quan và quan hệ nhân quả.

* **distinct** /dɪˈstɪŋkt/ *(adjective)*: riêng biệt, khác rõ  
  *Example:* The two populations occupy distinct ecological niches.  
  → Hai quần thể chiếm những ổ sinh thái riêng biệt.

* **distinctive** /dɪˈstɪŋk.tɪv/ *(adjective)*: đặc trưng, dễ nhận ra  
  *Example:* The building has a distinctive curved roof.  
  → Toà nhà có mái cong rất đặc trưng.

##### Notes

`Distinct` means separate or clearly different; `distinctive` means characteristic enough to identify something. `Distinguished` can also mean eminent, as in `a distinguished scientist`.

---

## Change and Development

| Scale/type | Common forms | Nuance |
| --- | --- | --- |
| general change | change, alter, modify | `Modify` normally means a limited or purposeful adjustment. |
| major change | transform, revolutionise | A fundamental change in form, function, or system. |
| gradual development | evolve, emerge, develop | `Emerge` focuses on becoming visible or established. |
| adjustment | adapt, adjust | `Adapt` often responds to conditions; `adjust` can be smaller. |
| replacement | replace, supersede, displace | `Supersede` means replace because the new form is considered better/newer. |

### Core anchor entry

#### 7. Transform /trænsˈfɔːm/

* **Part of speech:** transitive and intransitive verb
* **Vietnamese meaning:** biến đổi mạnh, chuyển hoá

##### Usage

`Transform` refers to a substantial change in form, condition, function, or character. It is stronger than `modify` and does not merely mean that one numerical value rises or falls.

**Common structures:**

* `transform A into B`: biến A thành B
* `transform the way + clause`: thay đổi căn bản cách...
* `be transformed by + factor`: được biến đổi bởi

##### Examples

1. **Railways transformed the way goods were transported between cities.**
   → Đường sắt đã làm thay đổi căn bản cách hàng hoá được vận chuyển giữa các thành phố.

2. **During metamorphosis, the larva is transformed into an adult insect.**
   → Trong quá trình biến thái, ấu trùng chuyển hoá thành côn trùng trưởng thành.

##### Common collocations

* **radically transform**: biến đổi triệt để
* **transform a system**: cải biến một hệ thống
* **digital transformation**: chuyển đổi số
* **social transformation**: biến đổi xã hội

##### Synonyms

* **alter** /ˈɔːl.tər/ *(verb)*: thay đổi; trung tính và thường nhỏ hơn
* **convert** /kənˈvɜːt/ *(verb)*: chuyển từ dạng, công dụng, hoặc hệ thống này sang dạng khác
* **revolutionise** /ˌrev.əˈluː.ʃən.aɪz/ *(verb)*: cách mạng hoá; nhấn mạnh thay đổi sâu sắc và mới mẻ

##### Word family

* **transformation** /ˌtræns.fəˈmeɪ.ʃən/ *(countable/uncountable noun)*: sự biến đổi  
  *Example:* The region underwent rapid economic transformation.  
  → Khu vực đã trải qua sự chuyển biến kinh tế nhanh chóng.

* **transformative** /trænsˈfɔː.mə.tɪv/ *(adjective)*: có khả năng tạo thay đổi lớn  
  *Example:* The invention had a transformative effect on agriculture.  
  → Phát minh có tác động mang tính chuyển đổi lớn đối với nông nghiệp.

##### Notes

Use `transform into` for the new form and `transform from A into B` when both stages are stated. `Transformation` does not automatically imply improvement; context determines whether the change is beneficial.

---

## Problems and Solutions

| Stage | Common forms | Typical objects |
| --- | --- | --- |
| identify | detect, identify, diagnose | a fault, pattern, disease, underlying cause |
| respond | address, tackle, confront | an issue, inequality, shortage, challenge |
| reduce severity | alleviate, mitigate, curb | pressure/pain; risk/impact; growth/emissions |
| remove | eliminate, eradicate | an error/barrier; a disease/pest |
| apply solution | implement, intervene, remedy | a policy; in a process; a defect/problem |

### Core anchor entry

#### 8. Address /əˈdres/

* **Part of speech:** transitive verb; countable noun (other senses)
* **Vietnamese meaning:** giải quyết, xem xét nghiêm túc *(problem sense)*; đề cập; phát biểu với

##### Usage

As an academic verb, `address` means to direct attention or action towards a problem, question, or need. It does **not** guarantee that the problem is solved. In passage summaries, `the author addresses X` may simply mean that X is discussed.

**Common structures:**

* `address a problem/issue/need`: xử lý một vấn đề/nhu cầu
* `address the question of + noun`: xem xét câu hỏi/vấn đề
* `measures designed to address + noun`: biện pháp nhằm xử lý

##### Examples

1. **The policy was introduced to address the shortage of affordable housing.**
   → Chính sách được đưa ra nhằm giải quyết tình trạng thiếu nhà ở vừa túi tiền.

2. **The final section addresses the limitations of the experiment.**
   → Phần cuối đề cập đến những hạn chế của thí nghiệm.

##### Common collocations

* **address an issue**: xử lý/đề cập một vấn đề
* **address a concern**: giải quyết một mối lo ngại
* **address the needs of**: đáp ứng nhu cầu của
* **directly address**: trực tiếp xử lý/đề cập

##### Synonyms

* **tackle** /ˈtæk.əl/ *(verb)*: xử lý tích cực một vấn đề khó; gợi nỗ lực trực tiếp
* **deal with** /diːl wɪð/ *(phrasal verb)*: xử lý/đề cập; rộng và ít trang trọng hơn
* **resolve** /rɪˈzɒlv/ *(verb)*: giải quyết thành công; mạnh hơn `address`

##### Word family

* **address** /ˈæd.res/ *(countable noun)*: địa chỉ; bài phát biểu  
  *Example:* The archive records the last known address of each participant.  
  → Kho lưu trữ ghi lại địa chỉ cuối cùng được biết của từng người tham gia.

* **addressee** /ˌæd.resˈiː/ *(countable noun)*: người nhận/người được nói tới  
  *Example:* Pronoun choice may depend on the relationship with the addressee.  
  → Việc chọn đại từ có thể phụ thuộc vào mối quan hệ với người nghe.

##### Notes

Do not infer `solve` from `address`. A programme may address a problem yet fail to remove it. The verb is pronounced /əˈdres/; the noun is commonly /ˈæd.res/ in British English.

---

## Processes

| Process relation | Common forms | Example frame |
| --- | --- | --- |
| experience | undergo, experience | `undergo a change/treatment` |
| consist of stages | comprise, consist of | `comprises three stages`; `consists of three stages` |
| change form | convert, transform | `convert heat into electricity` |
| movement | transfer, transmit, circulate | data/energy/material moves through a system |
| retention/removal | retain, release, extract | keep, let out, or take out a substance |

### Core anchor entries

#### 9. Undergo /ˌʌn.dəˈɡəʊ/

* **Part of speech:** transitive irregular verb (`underwent`, `undergone`)
* **Vietnamese meaning:** trải qua, chịu một quá trình hoặc thay đổi

##### Usage

`Undergo` is used when a person, organism, material, or system experiences a process, test, treatment, or significant change. The subject receives or experiences the process; it does not actively perform it.

**Common structures:**

* `undergo a change/transformation`: trải qua thay đổi
* `undergo testing/treatment`: được kiểm tra/điều trị
* `undergo a process`: trải qua một quá trình

##### Examples

1. **The samples underwent chemical analysis before being stored.**
   → Các mẫu đã được phân tích hoá học trước khi được lưu trữ.

2. **Many insects undergo complete metamorphosis.**
   → Nhiều loài côn trùng trải qua quá trình biến thái hoàn toàn.

##### Common collocations

* **undergo treatment**: trải qua điều trị
* **undergo testing**: được kiểm nghiệm
* **undergo rapid change**: trải qua thay đổi nhanh chóng
* **undergo renovation**: được cải tạo

##### Synonyms

* **experience** /ɪkˈspɪə.ri.əns/ *(verb)*: trải qua; rộng hơn và ít nhấn mạnh một quy trình được xác định
* **be subjected to** /biː səbˈdʒek.tɪd tuː/ *(passive phrase)*: phải chịu/được đặt dưới; thường gợi điều kiện hoặc kiểm tra áp lên đối tượng
* **go through** /ɡəʊ θruː/ *(phrasal verb)*: trải qua; ít trang trọng hơn

##### Word family

`Undergo` has no common derivational noun or adjective. Academic English normally uses the noun naming the process itself.

* **underwent** /ˌʌn.dəˈwent/ *(past tense)*: đã trải qua  
  *Example:* The bridge underwent extensive repairs in 2018.  
  → Cây cầu đã trải qua đợt sửa chữa lớn vào năm 2018.

* **undergone** /ˌʌn.dəˈɡɒn/ *(past participle)*: đã trải qua  
  *Example:* The landscape has undergone considerable erosion.  
  → Cảnh quan đã bị xói mòn đáng kể.

##### Notes

`Undergo` takes a direct object: `undergo an operation`, not `undergo through an operation`. Its passive form is normally unnecessary because the subject already experiences the action.

---

#### 10. Convert /kənˈvɜːt/

* **Part of speech:** transitive and intransitive verb
* **Vietnamese meaning:** chuyển đổi, biến đổi sang dạng/hệ/công dụng khác

##### Usage

`Convert` describes changing something into a different form, function, unit, belief system, or type. Process passages often track conversion of energy, materials, land, or data.

**Common structures:**

* `convert A into/to B`: chuyển A thành B
* `be converted from A into B`: được chuyển từ A thành B
* `convert between units/formats`: chuyển đổi giữa các đơn vị/định dạng

##### Examples

1. **Photovoltaic cells convert sunlight into electricity.**
   → Pin quang điện chuyển ánh sáng mặt trời thành điện năng.

2. **Part of the warehouse was converted into laboratory space.**
   → Một phần nhà kho được cải tạo thành không gian phòng thí nghiệm.

##### Common collocations

* **convert energy**: chuyển hoá năng lượng
* **convert land**: chuyển đổi mục đích sử dụng đất
* **convert a building**: cải tạo công năng toà nhà
* **unit conversion**: chuyển đổi đơn vị

##### Synonyms

* **transform** /trænsˈfɔːm/ *(verb)*: biến đổi lớn về đặc tính/hình thức; rộng hơn
* **change into** /tʃeɪndʒ ˈɪn.tuː/ *(verb phrase)*: biến thành; thông dụng hơn
* **adapt** /əˈdæpt/ *(verb)*: điều chỉnh cho mục đích hoặc điều kiện mới, không nhất thiết đổi loại hoàn toàn

##### Word family

* **conversion** /kənˈvɜː.ʃən/ *(countable/uncountable noun)*: sự chuyển đổi  
  *Example:* Energy conversion is never perfectly efficient.  
  → Việc chuyển đổi năng lượng không bao giờ đạt hiệu suất hoàn hảo.

* **convertible** /kənˈvɜː.tə.bəl/ *(adjective)*: có thể chuyển đổi  
  *Example:* The seats are convertible into sleeping platforms.  
  → Ghế có thể chuyển thành bệ ngủ.

* **converter** /kənˈvɜː.tər/ *(countable noun)*: bộ chuyển đổi  
  *Example:* A converter changes the electrical signal into sound.  
  → Bộ chuyển đổi biến tín hiệu điện thành âm thanh.

##### Notes

Both `convert into` and `convert to` occur, but `into` strongly highlights the resulting form. Do not confuse `convert` with `convey` (“transport or communicate”).

---


## Measurement and Quantities

| Quantity | Use | Common trap |
| --- | --- | --- |
| amount | uncountable nouns | `an amount of water`, not normally `an amount of people` |
| number | countable plural nouns | `the number of participants` takes a singular verb |
| proportion | a part relative to the whole | May be expressed as a fraction or percentage. |
| rate | change/events per unit of time or population | A high rate is not necessarily a large total number. |
| ratio | numerical relationship between two quantities | A 2:1 ratio is not the same as two percentage points. |
| extent | degree, scope, or area | Often used in `the extent to which`. |

### Core anchor entry

#### 11. Proportion /prəˈpɔː.ʃən/

* **Part of speech:** countable noun; uncountable noun in fixed expressions
* **Vietnamese meaning:** tỷ lệ/phần trong tổng thể *(75%)*; quy mô hoặc sự cân đối *(25%)*

##### Usage

A `proportion` is a part considered in relation to a whole, without requiring a specific format such as a percentage. It can also refer to relative size or balance. Reading questions often paraphrase `a large proportion of` as `many` or `the majority of`, but these are not always numerically identical.

**Common structures:**

* `the proportion of + plural/uncountable noun`: tỷ lệ của
* `a high/low proportion of`: một tỷ lệ cao/thấp
* `in proportion to + noun`: tương xứng với
* `a small proportion of the total`: một phần nhỏ trong tổng số

##### Examples

1. **A high proportion of the respondents travelled to work by bus.**
   → Một tỷ lệ lớn người trả lời khảo sát đi làm bằng xe buýt.

2. **The brain is large in proportion to the animal's body.**
   → Bộ não lớn so với tỷ lệ cơ thể của loài vật.

##### Common collocations

* **a significant proportion**: một tỷ lệ đáng kể
* **a growing proportion**: tỷ lệ ngày càng tăng
* **in direct proportion to**: tỷ lệ thuận với
* **out of proportion**: không cân xứng

##### Synonyms

* **percentage** /pəˈsen.tɪdʒ/ *(noun)*: tỷ lệ phần trăm; luôn biểu thị trên thang 100
* **share** /ʃeər/ *(noun)*: phần/tỷ phần; tự nhiên trong kinh tế và phân chia nguồn lực
* **fraction** /ˈfræk.ʃən/ *(noun)*: phần nhỏ/phân số; thường ngụ ý phần tương đối nhỏ trong văn xuôi

##### Word family

* **proportional** /prəˈpɔː.ʃən.əl/ *(adjective)*: theo tỷ lệ, cân xứng  
  *Example:* The tax is proportional to the value of the property.  
  → Thuế tỷ lệ thuận với giá trị bất động sản.

* **proportionally** /prəˈpɔː.ʃən.əl.i/ *(adverb)*: xét theo tỷ lệ  
  *Example:* Smaller animals lose proportionally more heat.  
  → Những động vật nhỏ mất nhiều nhiệt hơn nếu xét theo tỷ lệ.

* **disproportionate** /ˌdɪs.prəˈpɔː.ʃən.ət/ *(adjective)*: không cân xứng, quá lớn/nhỏ so với  
  *Example:* Drought has a disproportionate impact on poor households.  
  → Hạn hán gây tác động không cân xứng lên các hộ nghèo.

##### Notes

With `the proportion of`, the verb normally agrees with `proportion`: `the proportion ... has risen`. With `a proportion of + plural noun`, singular or plural agreement can depend on whether the writer emphasises the share or the people/things within it.

---

## Probability and Uncertainty

| Confidence | Common forms | Caution |
| --- | --- | --- |
| strong | almost certainly, highly likely | Still not the same as a proven fact. |
| moderate | likely, probable, appears to | `Likely` can be adjective; `probably` is the adverb. |
| possibility | may, might, could, possible | These often signal that alternatives remain. |
| tentative | apparently, seemingly, tentative | Based on incomplete or indirect evidence. |
| prediction | estimate, project, forecast | Each depends on assumptions and data. |

### Core anchor entry

#### 12. Estimate /ˈes.tɪ.meɪt/ (noun); /ˈes.tɪ.meɪt/ (verb)

* **Part of speech:** transitive verb; countable noun
* **Vietnamese meaning:** ước tính; con số/sự ước tính

##### Usage

To `estimate` is to calculate a value approximately when exact measurement is unavailable or unnecessary. An estimate should not be read as an exact count. Academic estimates may be based on models, samples, indirect indicators, or expert judgement.

**Common structures:**

* `estimate that + clause`: ước tính rằng
* `be estimated at + figure`: được ước tính ở mức
* `be estimated to be/have`: được ước tính là/có
* `an estimate of + quantity`: ước tính về

##### Examples

1. **The population is estimated to have reached 1.2 million by 1800.**
   → Dân số được ước tính đã đạt 1,2 triệu người vào năm 1800.

2. **Satellite images provide an estimate of the area affected by flooding.**
   → Ảnh vệ tinh cung cấp ước tính về diện tích bị ảnh hưởng bởi lũ lụt.

##### Common collocations

* **rough estimate**: ước tính sơ bộ
* **conservative estimate**: ước tính thận trọng
* **estimate the cost**: ước tính chi phí
* **estimated value**: giá trị ước tính

##### Synonyms

* **calculate** /ˈkæl.kjə.leɪt/ *(verb)*: tính toán; có thể cho kết quả chính xác bằng quy tắc/toán học
* **approximate** /əˈprɒk.sɪ.meɪt/ *(verb)*: xấp xỉ/ước lượng; nhấn mạnh độ gần đúng
* **project** /prəˈdʒekt/ *(verb)*: dự báo giá trị tương lai từ xu hướng/giả định

##### Word family

* **estimation** /ˌes.tɪˈmeɪ.ʃən/ *(noun)*: sự ước tính; sự đánh giá  
  *Example:* Accurate estimation is difficult when records are incomplete.  
  → Việc ước tính chính xác gặp khó khăn khi hồ sơ không đầy đủ.

* **estimated** /ˈes.tɪ.meɪ.tɪd/ *(adjective)*: được ước tính  
  *Example:* The estimated lifespan varies between populations.  
  → Tuổi thọ ước tính khác nhau giữa các quần thể.

* **underestimate** /ˌʌn.dərˈes.tɪ.meɪt/ *(verb)*: đánh giá/ước tính thấp  
  *Example:* Early surveys may have underestimated the number of species.  
  → Các khảo sát ban đầu có thể đã ước tính thấp số lượng loài.

##### Notes

`Estimated at` precedes a value (`costs were estimated at £2 million`); `estimated to` precedes a verb (`estimated to cost`). Watch hedging combinations such as `may be underestimated`, which contain two layers of uncertainty.

---

## Arguments and Claims

| Function | Common forms | Strength/stance |
| --- | --- | --- |
| present a position | argue, claim, contend, maintain | `Claim` may signal distance; `maintain` suggests continued belief. |
| support | justify, substantiate, defend | Give reasons or evidence for a position. |
| question | challenge, dispute, cast doubt on | Express disagreement without necessarily disproving. |
| reject | refute, disprove, rebut | `Disprove/refute` require strong counter-evidence; `rebut` answers an argument. |
| accept partly | acknowledge, concede | Recognise a point, often before presenting a contrast. |

### Core anchor entry

#### 13. Claim /kleɪm/

* **Part of speech:** transitive verb; countable noun
* **Vietnamese meaning:** tuyên bố/cho rằng *(without guaranteed proof)*; luận điểm; yêu cầu quyền lợi

##### Usage

In argumentative texts, a `claim` is a statement presented as true but still open to evaluation. The verb often lets a writer report someone else's position without endorsing it. In other contexts it can mean formally requesting something or asserting ownership.

**Common structures:**

* `claim that + clause`: cho rằng
* `claim to do/be/have`: tuyên bố làm/là/có
* `make/support/challenge a claim`: đưa ra/ủng hộ/phản bác luận điểm
* `a claim for/to + noun`: yêu cầu về/quyền đối với

##### Examples

1. **The researchers claim that the technique reduces measurement error.**
   → Các nhà nghiên cứu cho rằng kỹ thuật này làm giảm sai số đo lường.

2. **Later evidence challenged the claim that the settlement had been abandoned suddenly.**
   → Bằng chứng sau đó đã đặt nghi vấn đối với nhận định rằng khu định cư bị bỏ hoang đột ngột.

##### Common collocations

* **make a claim**: đưa ra một nhận định/yêu cầu
* **support a claim**: củng cố một luận điểm
* **unsubstantiated claim**: tuyên bố không có bằng chứng hỗ trợ
* **claim ownership**: tuyên bố quyền sở hữu

##### Synonyms

* **argue** /ˈɑː.ɡjuː/ *(verb)*: lập luận rằng; ngụ ý có lý do hoặc chuỗi lập luận
* **assert** /əˈsɜːt/ *(verb)*: khẳng định mạnh mẽ; không nhất thiết đưa bằng chứng
* **contend** /kənˈtend/ *(verb)*: cho rằng/tranh luận; trang trọng và thường gặp trong văn học thuật

##### Word family

* **claimant** /ˈkleɪ.mənt/ *(countable noun)*: người yêu cầu quyền lợi/bồi thường  
  *Example:* Each claimant had to provide documentary evidence.  
  → Mỗi người yêu cầu quyền lợi phải cung cấp bằng chứng giấy tờ.

* **claimed** /kleɪmd/ *(adjective/participle)*: được tuyên bố/được cho là  
  *Example:* The claimed benefit was not observed in the larger trial.  
  → Lợi ích được tuyên bố không xuất hiện trong thử nghiệm lớn hơn.

* **counterclaim** /ˈkaʊn.tə.kleɪm/ *(countable noun)*: phản biện/đơn kiện ngược  
  *Example:* The second paragraph presents a counterclaim.  
  → Đoạn thứ hai trình bày một lập luận phản bác.

##### Notes

Do not treat `X claims that...` as proof that X is correct. In matching-claims questions, track **who holds the view** as well as its content.

---

## Advantages and Disadvantages

| Positive | Negative | Useful contrast |
| --- | --- | --- |
| benefit, advantage, merit | drawback, disadvantage, limitation | A limitation restricts evidence or use; it is not always a harmful effect. |
| beneficial, advantageous | detrimental, adverse | `Adverse` commonly modifies effects, conditions, or reactions. |
| outweigh | offset, counteract | Benefits may outweigh costs; one effect may offset another. |

### Core anchor entry

#### 14. Outweigh /ˌaʊtˈweɪ/

* **Part of speech:** transitive verb
* **Vietnamese meaning:** quan trọng/lớn hơn; vượt trội khi cân nhắc

##### Usage

`Outweigh` compares the relative importance, value, or size of two opposing factors. It does not mean that the weaker factor disappears. Academic arguments commonly ask whether benefits outweigh costs or whether risks outweigh potential gains.

**Common structures:**

* `A outweighs B`: A lớn/quan trọng hơn B
* `benefits outweigh the costs/risks`: lợi ích lớn hơn chi phí/rủi ro
* `be outweighed by + noun`: bị lấn át bởi

##### Examples

1. **For remote communities, the benefits of the network may outweigh its construction costs.**
   → Đối với các cộng đồng xa xôi, lợi ích của mạng lưới có thể lớn hơn chi phí xây dựng.

2. **A small increase in accuracy was outweighed by the longer processing time.**
   → Mức tăng nhỏ về độ chính xác bị lấn át bởi thời gian xử lý dài hơn.

##### Common collocations

* **outweigh the disadvantages**: lớn hơn các bất lợi
* **far outweigh**: vượt xa
* **risks outweigh benefits**: rủi ro lớn hơn lợi ích
* **be outweighed by**: bị lấn át bởi

##### Synonyms

* **exceed** /ɪkˈsiːd/ *(verb)*: vượt quá về số lượng/mức giới hạn; không nhất thiết là cân nhắc giá trị
* **override** /ˌəʊ.vəˈraɪd/ *(verb)*: lấn át hoặc bác bỏ một quyết định/yếu tố
* **be more significant than** /biː mɔː sɪɡˈnɪf.ɪ.kənt ðæn/ *(phrase)*: đáng kể/quan trọng hơn; diễn đạt tường minh

##### Word family

`Outweigh` is built from `out-` (“surpass”) + `weigh`; it has no useful derived noun.

* **weight** /weɪt/ *(noun)*: trọng lượng; tầm quan trọng  
  *Example:* The review gives greater weight to evidence from controlled trials.  
  → Bài tổng quan coi trọng hơn bằng chứng từ các thử nghiệm có đối chứng.

* **weighty** /ˈweɪ.ti/ *(adjective)*: quan trọng, nghiêm túc  
  *Example:* The committee considered several weighty objections.  
  → Uỷ ban đã xem xét một số phản đối quan trọng.

##### Notes

The direct-object pattern is `A outweighs B`, not `A outweighs than B`. A sentence with `may outweigh` presents an evaluation, not a measured fact.

---

## Classification

| Function | Common forms | Grammar |
| --- | --- | --- |
| group | classify, categorise, group | `classify A as B`; `group A according to B` |
| membership | belong to, fall into | category follows the preposition |
| composition | comprise, consist of, constitute | Whole comprises parts; parts constitute whole. |
| rule | criterion, basis, characteristic | plural of `criterion` is `criteria` |

### Core anchor entry

#### 15. Constitute /ˈkɒn.stɪ.tjuːt/

* **Part of speech:** transitive verb
* **Vietnamese meaning:** cấu thành; chiếm (một tỷ lệ); được xem là

##### Usage

`Constitute` can mean that parts form a whole, that something represents a proportion of a total, or that an act counts as a particular category. It is common in classification, demographic, legal, and scientific writing.

**Common structures:**

* `A and B constitute C`: A và B cấu thành C
* `constitute + percentage/proportion`: chiếm một tỷ lệ
* `constitute a threat/change/breach`: tạo thành/được xem là

##### Examples

1. **Organic matter constitutes only a small proportion of the sediment.**
   → Chất hữu cơ chỉ chiếm một tỷ lệ nhỏ trong lớp trầm tích.

2. **These three stages constitute the complete production cycle.**
   → Ba giai đoạn này tạo thành toàn bộ chu trình sản xuất.

##### Common collocations

* **constitute a majority**: chiếm đa số
* **constitute evidence**: được xem là bằng chứng
* **constitute a threat**: tạo thành mối đe doạ
* **constitute part of**: tạo thành một phần của

##### Synonyms

* **form** /fɔːm/ *(verb)*: tạo thành; trung tính và rộng hơn
* **make up** /meɪk ʌp/ *(phrasal verb)*: cấu thành/chiếm; ít trang trọng hơn
* **comprise** /kəmˈpraɪz/ *(verb)*: bao gồm; chuẩn nhất khi chủ ngữ là toàn thể và tân ngữ là các phần

##### Word family

* **constituent** /kənˈstɪtʃ.u.ənt/ *(countable noun/adjective)*: thành phần cấu tạo  
  *Example:* Nitrogen is the largest constituent of Earth's atmosphere.  
  → Nitơ là thành phần lớn nhất của khí quyển Trái Đất.

* **constitution** /ˌkɒn.stɪˈtjuː.ʃən/ *(noun)*: hiến pháp; cấu tạo/thể trạng  
  *Example:* The constitution defines the powers of the institution.  
  → Hiến pháp xác định quyền hạn của thể chế.

* **constitutional** /ˌkɒn.stɪˈtjuː.ʃən.əl/ *(adjective)*: thuộc hiến pháp/thể chất  
  *Example:* The reform raised constitutional questions.  
  → Cuộc cải cách đặt ra các vấn đề về hiến pháp.

##### Notes

Direction matters: `the parts constitute the whole`, but `the whole comprises/consists of the parts`. Avoid the disputed construction `is comprised of` in careful formal writing; use `is composed of` or `comprises`.

---

## Time and Chronology

| Order | Common forms | Note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| before | prior to, precede, formerly | `Prior to` takes a noun/gerund; `precede` is a verb. |
| after | subsequent to, follow, thereafter | `Subsequent` is formal and relational. |
| same time | simultaneous, concurrent, meanwhile | Concurrent events overlap but need not begin together. |
| stages | initially, subsequently, eventually | `Eventually` means finally after time, not “possibly”. |
| duration | temporary, persistent, continual, continuous | `Continual` = repeated; `continuous` = without interruption. |

### Core anchor entry

#### 16. Precede /prɪˈsiːd/

* **Part of speech:** transitive verb
* **Vietnamese meaning:** xảy ra/đứng trước

##### Usage

`Precede` means to occur, exist, or appear before something else in time, order, or position. It expresses sequence, not causation: if A preceded B, A did not necessarily cause B.

**Common structures:**

* `A precedes B`: A xảy ra/đứng trước B
* `be preceded by + noun`: có ... xảy ra trước
* `in the years preceding + event`: trong những năm trước sự kiện

##### Examples

1. **A prolonged drought preceded the collapse of the settlement.**
   → Một đợt hạn hán kéo dài xảy ra trước sự sụp đổ của khu định cư.

2. **Each trial was preceded by a short practice session.**
   → Trước mỗi lượt thử nghiệm đều có một buổi thực hành ngắn.

##### Common collocations

* **immediately precede**: xảy ra ngay trước
* **preceding period**: giai đoạn trước đó
* **precede an event**: xảy ra trước một sự kiện
* **be preceded by**: được diễn ra trước bởi

##### Synonyms

* **come before** /kʌm bɪˈfɔː/ *(verb phrase)*: đến/xảy ra trước; thông dụng hơn
* **predate** /ˌpriːˈdeɪt/ *(verb)*: có từ trước một thời điểm/vật khác, thường cách khá lâu
* **lead up to** /liːd ʌp tuː/ *(phrasal verb)*: diễn ra trong giai đoạn trước và có liên hệ tới sự kiện sau

##### Word family

* **preceding** /prɪˈsiː.dɪŋ/ *(adjective)*: trước đó  
  *Example:* Rainfall was lower than in the preceding decade.  
  → Lượng mưa thấp hơn so với thập kỷ trước đó.

* **precedent** /ˈpres.ɪ.dənt/ *(countable noun)*: tiền lệ  
  *Example:* The ruling established a precedent for later cases.  
  → Phán quyết tạo tiền lệ cho các vụ việc sau này.

* **unprecedented** /ʌnˈpres.ɪ.den.tɪd/ *(adjective)*: chưa từng có tiền lệ  
  *Example:* The city experienced unprecedented population growth.  
  → Thành phố trải qua mức tăng dân số chưa từng có.

##### Notes

Do not confuse `precede` /prɪˈsiːd/ with `proceed` /prəˈsiːd/ (“continue”). `Precedence` means priority; `precedent` is an earlier case used as an example.

---

## Relationships and Correlations

| Relationship | Common forms | Interpretation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| neutral link | relate to, be associated with, correspond to | Does not alone establish cause. |
| measured co-variation | correlate with, correlation | Variables change together statistically. |
| dependence | depend on, be contingent on | One outcome varies according to another condition. |
| interaction | interact, reinforce, counteract | Factors act on one another rather than separately. |

### Core anchor entry

#### 17. Correlate /ˈkɒr.ə.leɪt/ (verb); /ˈkɒr.ə.lət/ (noun)

* **Part of speech:** intransitive/transitive verb; countable noun (technical)
* **Vietnamese meaning:** có tương quan, đối chiếu mối tương quan; yếu tố tương quan

##### Usage

Two variables `correlate` when they vary together in a systematic way. A positive correlation means they move in the same direction; a negative correlation means they move in opposite directions. Correlation alone does not demonstrate that either variable causes the other.

**Common structures:**

* `A correlates with B`: A tương quan với B
* `be positively/negatively correlated with`: có tương quan thuận/nghịch với
* `correlate A with B`: đối chiếu A với B

##### Examples

1. **Vocabulary size correlates positively with reading comprehension.**
   → Vốn từ có tương quan thuận với khả năng đọc hiểu.

2. **The researchers correlated satellite measurements with observations on the ground.**
   → Các nhà nghiên cứu đối chiếu số đo vệ tinh với quan sát thực địa.

##### Common collocations

* **strongly correlate**: có tương quan mạnh
* **positive correlation**: tương quan thuận
* **inverse correlation**: tương quan nghịch
* **correlate data with outcomes**: đối chiếu dữ liệu với kết quả

##### Synonyms

* **be associated with** /biː əˈsəʊ.si.eɪ.tɪd wɪð/ *(phrase)*: có liên hệ với; rộng hơn và không nhất thiết là hệ số thống kê
* **correspond to/with** /ˌkɒr.ɪˈspɒnd/ *(verb)*: tương ứng/phù hợp; thường nhấn mạnh sự khớp nhau
* **relate to** /rɪˈleɪt tuː/ *(verb)*: liên quan đến; khái quát hơn

##### Word family

* **correlation** /ˌkɒr.əˈleɪ.ʃən/ *(countable/uncountable noun)*: mối tương quan  
  *Example:* The study found a correlation but no direct causal mechanism.  
  → Nghiên cứu tìm thấy mối tương quan nhưng không có cơ chế nhân quả trực tiếp.

* **correlated** /ˈkɒr.ə.leɪ.tɪd/ *(adjective/participle)*: có tương quan  
  *Example:* The two measures are highly correlated.  
  → Hai thước đo có tương quan cao.

* **correlational** /ˌkɒr.əˈleɪ.ʃən.əl/ *(adjective)*: thuộc nghiên cứu tương quan  
  *Example:* A correlational design cannot by itself establish causality.  
  → Thiết kế nghiên cứu tương quan tự nó không thể xác lập quan hệ nhân quả.

##### Notes

The standard preposition is `correlate with`. In IELTS questions, replacing `is associated with` by `causes` is often an unjustified strengthening and may make a statement False or Not Given.

---

## Evaluation

| Question | Useful vocabulary | Focus |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Does it work? | effective, successful, valid | achievement of intended result |
| Does it waste resources? | efficient, economical | output relative to time, energy, or cost |
| Can it be done? | feasible, viable, practicable | real-world possibility and sustainability |
| Can it be trusted? | reliable, robust, consistent | repeatability and resistance to weakness |
| How important is it? | significant, substantial, negligible | size or importance; statistical `significant` is technical |

### Core anchor entry

#### 18. Feasible /ˈfiː.zə.bəl/

* **Part of speech:** adjective
* **Vietnamese meaning:** khả thi, có thể thực hiện được trên thực tế

##### Usage

`Feasible` evaluates whether a plan, method, or solution can realistically be carried out with available knowledge, time, money, or resources. Something may be technically possible but not economically feasible.

**Common structures:**

* `it is feasible to + verb`: có thể thực hiện việc...
* `a feasible solution/alternative`: giải pháp/phương án khả thi
* `technically/economically feasible`: khả thi về kỹ thuật/kinh tế

##### Examples

1. **Desalination is technically feasible but remains costly in many regions.**
   → Khử mặn khả thi về kỹ thuật nhưng vẫn tốn kém ở nhiều khu vực.

2. **The pilot study examined whether large-scale monitoring was feasible.**
   → Nghiên cứu thí điểm xem xét liệu việc giám sát quy mô lớn có khả thi hay không.

##### Common collocations

* **feasible option**: phương án khả thi
* **economically feasible**: khả thi về kinh tế
* **feasibility study**: nghiên cứu tính khả thi
* **technically feasible**: khả thi về kỹ thuật

##### Synonyms

* **viable** /ˈvaɪ.ə.bəl/ *(adjective)*: có khả năng hoạt động/thành công bền vững; thường mạnh hơn về triển vọng lâu dài
* **practicable** /ˈpræk.tɪ.kə.bəl/ *(adjective)*: có thể thực hiện trong thực tế; trang trọng
* **possible** /ˈpɒs.ə.bəl/ *(adjective)*: có thể; rộng hơn và không đảm bảo tính thực tế

##### Word family

* **feasibility** /ˌfiː.zəˈbɪl.ə.ti/ *(uncountable noun)*: tính khả thi  
  *Example:* Engineers assessed the feasibility of constructing a tunnel.  
  → Các kỹ sư đánh giá tính khả thi của việc xây đường hầm.

* **infeasible** /ɪnˈfiː.zə.bəl/ *(adjective)*: không khả thi  
  *Example:* The original timetable proved infeasible.  
  → Tiến độ ban đầu được chứng minh là không khả thi.

##### Notes

`Feasible` normally describes a proposal or action, not a person. Do not confuse `feasible` with `effective`: a feasible plan can be implemented, but it may still fail to achieve its goal.

---

# IELTS Reading Paraphrase Bank

Use this bank from **meaning to wording**. The items in a row overlap, but they are not automatically interchangeable. Grammar, strength, direction, and scope must still match the passage.

## Causes, Results, and Influence

| Meaning | Common forms in passages and questions | Distinction to notice |
| --- | --- | --- |
| cause | cause, lead to, result in, bring about, give rise to, trigger | `Trigger` initiates; `give rise to` often describes emergence. |
| come from | result from, arise from, stem from, be attributable to | Direction is result → source. |
| partial cause | contribute to, play a role/part in, be a factor in | Weaker than sole/direct causation. |
| explain a share | account for, explain, be responsible for | `Account for 30%` can mean constitute, not cause. |
| affect | influence, affect, have an impact/effect on, shape | `Shape` often means influence development over time. |
| prevent | prevent, inhibit, hinder, impede, obstruct | `Inhibit` restrains; `prevent` can mean stop entirely. |

## Increases, Decreases, and Stability

| Meaning | Common forms | Distinction to notice |
| --- | --- | --- |
| increase | rise, grow, climb, expand, escalate, surge | `Surge` is sudden; `escalate` often concerns problems/costs. |
| make greater | increase, raise, boost, enhance | `Enhance` means improve quality/effectiveness, not merely quantity. |
| decrease | decline, fall, drop, diminish, shrink | `Diminish` often concerns degree/importance; `shrink` concerns size. |
| make smaller | reduce, lower, curb, cut, lessen | `Curb` means control/restrain, not necessarily reverse. |
| stop rising/falling | level off, plateau, stabilise | A plateau follows change; `remain stable` may involve no prior change. |
| move irregularly | fluctuate, vary, oscillate | `Vary` can also mean differ across groups rather than over time. |
| disappear | vanish, cease, die out, become extinct | A decline is not disappearance; `cease` applies to an activity/process. |

## Importance, Size, and Degree

| Meaning | Common forms | Distinction to notice |
| --- | --- | --- |
| important | significant, important, crucial, vital, essential, pivotal | `Essential` means necessary; `significant` may mean notable or statistical. |
| large | considerable, substantial, extensive, marked | `Extensive` concerns range/area; `marked` means clearly noticeable. |
| small | slight, modest, marginal, negligible | `Negligible` means too small to matter, stronger than `slight`. |
| main | primary, principal, dominant, leading | `Primary` means main, not necessarily only. |
| widespread | prevalent, pervasive, ubiquitous, widespread | `Ubiquitous` means apparently everywhere; `prevalent` means common. |
| enough | sufficient, adequate | `Adequate` often means acceptable rather than abundant. |

## Showing, Finding, and Arguing

| Meaning | Common forms | Distinction to notice |
| --- | --- | --- |
| show | indicate, demonstrate, reveal, illustrate, display | `Illustrate` may give an example rather than prove a claim. |
| find | discover, identify, detect, observe, establish | `Detect` implies noticing something not easily visible. |
| say formally | state, report, note, observe, remark | `Observe` can mean notice or comment, depending on grammar. |
| argue | argue, contend, maintain, assert, claim | `Claim` and `assert` need not signal the writer's agreement. |
| support | support, substantiate, corroborate, bear out | `Corroborate` means independent evidence agrees. |
| oppose | challenge, dispute, contradict, rebut, refute | `Challenge` questions; `refute` successfully disproves. |
| infer | infer, deduce, conclude | The reader/researcher infers; evidence implies. |

## Similarity, Difference, and Classification

| Meaning | Common forms | Distinction to notice |
| --- | --- | --- |
| similar | similar, comparable, analogous, akin to | `Analogous` means similar in a relevant relation/function. |
| different | different, distinct, contrasting, divergent | `Distinct` can mean separate; `distinctive` means characteristic. |
| compare | compare, contrast, draw a parallel, set against | `Contrast` foregrounds differences. |
| type | type, kind, form, category, class | Categories depend on a stated or implied criterion. |
| group | classify, categorise, group, divide into | Watch whether the passage groups by origin, function, or appearance. |
| include | include, contain, encompass, incorporate | `Encompass` stresses broad coverage; `incorporate` means include as part. |
| consist of | comprise, consist of, be composed of | `Comprises` takes parts directly; `consists` requires `of`. |

## Change, Adaptation, and Replacement

| Meaning | Common forms | Distinction to notice |
| --- | --- | --- |
| change | alter, modify, shift, transform | `Modify` is limited; `transform` is fundamental. |
| develop | develop, evolve, emerge, progress | Evolution is change over generations/time, not automatically improvement. |
| adjust | adapt, adjust, accommodate | `Accommodate` can mean make room for a need/difference. |
| replace | replace, displace, supersede, substitute | `Displace` may force something out; `supersede` often implies a newer system. |
| preserve | preserve, conserve, safeguard, protect | `Conserve` often stresses careful resource use; `preserve` maintains condition. |
| restore | restore, rehabilitate, regenerate, revive | Each implies return, but the object and degree differ. |

## Problems, Solutions, and Evaluation

| Meaning | Common forms | Distinction to notice |
| --- | --- | --- |
| problem | issue, challenge, obstacle, barrier, constraint | An `issue` can be a neutral subject; a barrier blocks access/progress. |
| solve/respond | solve, resolve, address, tackle, remedy | `Address` and `tackle` do not guarantee success. |
| reduce harm | mitigate, alleviate, ease, offset | `Offset` counterbalances; it may not reduce the original source. |
| possible in practice | feasible, viable, practicable | `Viable` often implies capacity for continued success. |
| works | effective, successful | `Effective` concerns result; `efficient` concerns resources used. |
| weakness | limitation, shortcoming, drawback, flaw | A methodological limitation is not necessarily an error. |
| improve | improve, enhance, refine, optimise | `Refine` makes precise; `optimise` seeks the best attainable performance. |

## Amount, Frequency, and Distribution

| Meaning | Common forms | Distinction to notice |
| --- | --- | --- |
| most | most, the majority, the largest proportion | `Most` means more than half; `largest group` might still be below half. |
| some | some, a proportion, several, a number of | None of these automatically means many. |
| few | few, a minority, a small fraction/proportion | `Few` stresses scarcity; `a few` means some exist. |
| often | often, frequently, commonly, regularly | None means always. |
| everywhere/many places | throughout, across, widely distributed | `Throughout` can refer to an entire period as well as an area. |
| approximately | about, roughly, approximately, in the region of | These explicitly allow imprecision. |
| respectively | respectively, in the same order | Map each item to its partner in the stated sequence. |

## Time and Sequence

| Meaning | Common forms | Distinction to notice |
| --- | --- | --- |
| before | before, prior to, previously, formerly, preceding | `Formerly` describes an earlier state; `preceding` gives relative order. |
| after | after, following, subsequent to, thereafter | `Following` can be a preposition or adjective. |
| at first | initially, originally, at the outset | `Originally` may describe purpose/origin, not just the first stage. |
| in the end | eventually, ultimately, finally | `Eventually` means after time; it does not mean “possibly”. |
| at the same time | simultaneously, concurrently, meanwhile | `Meanwhile` may contrast parallel developments. |
| short-lived | temporary, brief, transient | `Transient` often describes a state that passes quickly. |
| lasting | persistent, sustained, enduring | `Persistent` can imply resistance to removal. |

## Location, Movement, and Spread

| Meaning | Common forms | Distinction to notice |
| --- | --- | --- |
| spread | spread, disperse, diffuse, disseminate | `Disseminate` normally concerns information; `disperse` concerns entities/particles. |
| move | move, shift, migrate, relocate | Migration can be seasonal/cyclical; relocation need not be. |
| near | adjacent, neighbouring, in proximity to | `Adjacent` means directly next to. |
| far | remote, distant, isolated | `Isolated` stresses weak connection as well as distance. |
| inside/outside | internal/external, interior/exterior | `Internal` and `external` often describe systems, causes, or validity. |

## High-Risk Meaning Contrasts

| Passage wording | Unsafe over-paraphrase | Why it changes the meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `may/can` | `will/does` | possibility becomes certainty |
| `is associated with` | `causes` | relationship becomes causation |
| `contributes to` | `is the sole cause of` | partial cause becomes exclusive cause |
| `reduces` | `eliminates/prevents` | degree reduction becomes complete removal |
| `some` | `most/all` | quantity is strengthened |
| `often/tends to` | `always` | tendency becomes universal rule |
| `one of the main factors` | `the main/only factor` | one member becomes uniquely dominant |
| `not all` | `none` | partial negation becomes total negation |
| `before` | `because of` | chronology becomes causation |
| `higher than` | `twice as high as` | vague comparison becomes exact magnitude |
| `estimated` | `measured exactly` | approximation becomes exact observation |
| `the largest group` | `a majority` | largest category may still contain under 50% |
| `failed to show` | `proved there was no` | absence of evidence becomes evidence of absence |
| `new for this region` | `new worldwide` | local scope becomes universal scope |

# Useful Academic Prefixes, Roots, and Suffixes

Word parts are **clues**, not complete definitions. Use them with sentence grammar, nearby examples, contrasts, and the topic context.

## Subject roots

| Root | Core meaning | Examples | How it helps |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `bio-` | life | biology, biodiversity, biodegradable | Signals living systems or breakdown by living organisms. |
| `geo-` | Earth/land | geology, geography, geothermal | Often links a term to Earth, land, or heat from Earth. |
| `eco-` | environment/home | ecology, ecosystem, ecotourism | Usually signals relations between organisms and their environment. |
| `neuro-` | nerve/nervous system | neuroscience, neurological, neuroplasticity | Locates a process in the brain or nervous system. |
| `anthrop-` | human | anthropology, anthropogenic | `Anthropogenic` means caused by human activity. |
| `psych-` | mind | psychology, psychological | Signals mental processes or behaviour. |
| `hydro-` | water | hydrology, hydroelectric, hydroponics | Connects a process to water. |
| `astro-` | star/space | astronomy, astrophysics | Signals celestial or space-related study. |
| `archaeo-` | ancient | archaeology, archaeobotany | Signals study of the human past through remains. |
| `demo-` | people/population | demography, demographic | In social science, relates to populations. |

## High-value prefixes

| Prefix | Meaning | Examples | Reading use |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `inter-` | between | interaction, interdependent, international | Two or more groups/systems relate across boundaries. |
| `intra-` | within | intracellular, intragroup | The relationship stays inside one unit/group. |
| `trans-` | across/change | transmit, transport, transformation | Movement across or change from one state to another. |
| `over-` | excessive/above | overuse, overestimate, overpopulation | More than an appropriate or actual level. |
| `under-` | insufficient/below | underestimate, underfunded, underrepresented | Less than needed, expected, or actual. |
| `pre-` | before | prehistoric, pre-existing, precondition | Earlier in time or logically required first. |
| `post-` | after | postwar, post-industrial | Later than a named event/period. |
| `re-` | again/back | reproduce, restore, regenerate | Repetition or return; exact meaning varies by base. |
| `de-` | remove/reverse/reduce | deforestation, decompose, decentralise | Loss, reversal, or movement away from a state. |
| `anti-` | against | antibiotic, anti-inflammatory | Opposes, prevents, or acts against. |
| `mono-` | one | monoculture, monolingual | One type/system. |
| `multi-/poly-` | many | multilingual, biodiversity, polymer | Multiple elements or types. |
| `micro-/macro-` | small/large scale | microorganism, microeconomics, macrostructure | Helps locate the scale of analysis. |

## High-value suffixes

| Suffix | Typical role | Examples | Caution |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `-ology` | field of study | ecology, geology, epidemiology | A person is usually an `-ologist`; adjective often ends `-ological`. |
| `-tion/-sion` | process/result noun | adaptation, conversion, expansion | Stress and spelling may change from the verb. |
| `-ity` | state/quality noun | diversity, validity, feasibility | Often built from an adjective: `valid → validity`. |
| `-ance/-ence` | state/process noun | resistance, occurrence, prevalence | Do not infer countability from the suffix alone. |
| `-ive` | adjective with active/tending sense | adaptive, effective, productive | Meaning depends on the base; `effective ≠ efficient`. |
| `-al` | relational adjective | environmental, cultural, neural | Often means “relating to”. |
| `-ous` | adjective “having/full of” | hazardous, porous, indigenous | The base may change spelling. |
| `-able/-ible` | capable of | sustainable, reversible, feasible | Can express practical or theoretical possibility. |
| `-ise` | make/become (verb) | urbanise, stabilise, categorise | British IELTS spelling; American English often uses `-ize`. |
| `-er/-or` | person/device that acts | researcher, pollinator, converter | The referent may be a person, organism, machine, or causal factor. |

## Productive word-family patterns

* `adapt → adaptation → adaptive`: động từ → quá trình/kết quả → tính chất.
* `analyse → analysis → analytical`: note the irregular plural `analyses` /əˈnæl.ə.siːz/.
* `vary → variation → variable`: `variable` may be a noun in research or an adjective meaning changeable.
* `signify → significance → significant`: `significant` can mean important/large enough to notice or statistically unlikely under a model.
* `economy → economic → economical`: `economic` relates to the economy; `economical` means not wasteful.
* `industry → industrial → industrious`: `industrial` relates to industry; `industrious` means hard-working.
* `history → historic → historical`: `historic` is important in history; `historical` simply relates to the past.

# Recommended Study Order

## Phase 1 — Highest Priority: argument language and recurring science

Study the Cross-Topic section, then Topics **4, 5, 2, 3, and 1** (research; psychology; ecology; biology; environment). These chapters give the greatest transfer: hypothesis and evidence, cause versus correlation, processes, comparison, adaptation, and change. Learn all Core entries and practise recognising their paraphrases before adding technical terms.

## Phase 2 — High Priority: recurring human and applied fields

Study Topics **8, 7, 6, 13, 9, 10, 11, and 12** (history; technology; health; cities; engineering; education; language; agriculture). Rotate fields rather than completing every term in one field at once. This interleaving prevents familiarity with a subject from being mistaken for reading skill.

## Phase 3 — Expansion: quantitative and interpretive breadth

Study Topics **14–18** (society; Earth science; economics; culture; astronomy). Focus on demographic comparison, physical processes, incentives, authors' interpretations, and evidence limitations. Move Advanced / Recognition items into active study only if they recur in your practice passages.

## Phase 4 — Advanced recognition and lower-frequency fields

Study Topics **19–20**, then review every Recognition bank. At this point the goal is rapid inference from roots, collocations, definitions, and examples—not perfect recall of every translation.

## A realistic twelve-week rotation

| Weeks | Main work | Review target |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1–2 | Cross-topic vocabulary + Topic 4 | causation, evidence strength, trends, quantities |
| 3–4 | Topics 5, 2, and 3 | psychology, ecology, biological processes |
| 5–6 | Topics 1, 8, and 7 | environmental arguments, chronology, technology |
| 7–8 | Topics 6, 13, and 9 | health evidence, cities, engineering processes |
| 9–10 | Topics 10, 11, 12, and 14 | education, language, agriculture, demographics |
| 11 | Topics 15–18 | Earth, economics, culture, space |
| 12 | Topics 19–20 + global review | recognition banks and weak paraphrase families |

# How to Study These Words

## 1. Train recognition before production

For Reading, first learn to recognise the headword, its common inflections, and its meaning **inside a sentence**. You do not need to use every Advanced item in speaking or writing. Mark each item as:

* **A — active:** you can use it accurately;
* **R — recognition:** you understand it quickly in context;
* **N — new/unstable:** it still slows comprehension.

Most Core words should eventually become A or strong R; many Advanced items can remain R.

## 2. Make two-sided cards that test meaning relationships

Avoid a card containing only `word = Vietnamese translation`. Better cards include:

* **Front:** one sentence with the word hidden, plus the topic.
* **Back:** headword + IPA, concise Vietnamese meaning, key structure, one collocation, and a contrast (`contribute to` is weaker than `cause`).

For a second card, reverse the relationship: `passage: stem from → question: be caused by`.

## 3. Learn word families as grammar signals

Underline the suffix and predict the grammatical slot before translating: `significant` fits before a noun; `significance` can be a subject/object; `significantly` modifies a verb, adjective, or clause. Record only useful family members and one natural sentence for each.

## 4. Learn collocations, not interchangeable synonyms

Store `pose a threat`, `conduct research`, `draw a conclusion`, `undergo treatment`, and `a decline in`. When two words appear similar, note one contrast:

* `effective` = achieves the goal; `efficient` = uses few resources;
* `economic` = related to the economy; `economical` = avoids waste;
* `adapt` = respond to conditions; `adopt` = begin using/accept;
* `historic` = historically important; `historical` = related to history.

## 5. Build a passage-to-question paraphrase log

After marking a test, use four columns:

| Passage wording | Question wording | Relationship | Why the wrong option fails |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `was associated with` | `was caused by` | unjustified strengthening | correlation was changed to causation |
| `a minority` | `some` | safe generalisation in that context | `most` would contradict the quantity |

The final column is essential: it turns a vocabulary list into question-solving skill.

## 6. Use spaced retrieval

Review new items after roughly **1 day, 3 days, 7 days, 14 days, and 30 days**, adjusting intervals to performance. A review should require recall or discrimination, not rereading. Mix topics so that you must retrieve meaning without relying on the chapter heading.

## 7. Recycle words across passages

For every practice passage:

1. Predict the field and activate 5–10 relevant terms.
2. Circle word families and box connectors showing cause, contrast, or concession.
3. Match each difficult question phrase to evidence in the text.
4. Add no more than 8–12 genuinely useful new items to review.
5. Revisit the same items in a second passage from a different field.

## 8. Use definitions before a dictionary when possible

IELTS passages may define necessary technical terms through apposition, brackets, examples, dashes, or a following clause. First identify the word's part of speech, root, contrast markers, and local definition. Then verify with a learner's dictionary. This trains the inference skill required under time pressure.

# Final Checklist

Before considering a chapter learned, check that you can:

* identify its most common passage subtopics without relying on specialist knowledge;
* explain the difference between its central processes and relationships;
* recognise every Core headword and its main word-family forms;
* understand Important items in a new sentence within a few seconds;
* infer many Advanced / Recognition items from roots and context;
* match at least three passage expressions to alternative question wording;
* distinguish correlation from causation, possibility from certainty, and reduction from elimination;
* notice restrictive quantifiers such as `some`, `most`, `only`, and `not all`;
* locate the exact sentence evidence before answering;
* review errors by paraphrase type rather than merely recording the correct letter.

The guide is complete as a reference, but your personal version should remain selective: promote recurring words to active study, keep transparent technical terms at recognition level, and add real paraphrase pairs from every Cambridge or official practice passage you complete.
